## 🔒 STANDING RULES (from the user — apply to EVERY agent, every session)

> Full version: [`AGENT_RULES.md`](AGENT_RULES.md). Summary:

- **This is the Saltiz app's football game.** Game code here (`football-mock`); app side in `pikmeTV-app` / `pikmeTV-saltiz` / `pikme-server`.
- **Multiple agents work this repo at once.** Assume someone else is editing — check `git status` / `git diff` before touching a file, say which files you're on, never revert or stomp work you didn't write.
- **Write down every user request here**, in short simple bullets, so any other agent can pick it up. Log it even if you didn't finish it (agent name, what was asked, what you changed, how you verified, status).
- **Commit everything locally.** Every finished piece of work gets a local commit — no work left dangling uncommitted for the next agent to guess at.
- **Never push unless the user asks.** `git push` / Render deploy only on an explicit "push" / "deploy".
- Local test server is `PORT=3012 node server.js` (main dev server is :3010). Node does NOT hot-reload — **restart the server** after server/shared changes.
- Before claiming done: `node --check` the files + run the test suite (`for f in test*.mjs; do node $f; done`); report real output, list known pre-existing fails separately.

So the pitch tutorial really did start — and the kid was looking at a full-screen «יפה מאוד! / עוד פעם» over it, unable to see or reach the lesson. Photographed: `_tu-shots/probe-after-finish.png`.

  - **A REPLAY's finish panel now has «סגירה».** It is full-screen and «עוד פעם» restarts the tour, so without a close it trapped the player on the lobby — the same mistake as a lesson with no skip.
  - **⚠️ SECOND BUG, found in the hand-off screenshot:** `finish()` removed `hub-tu-gate` but **left `ht-tour` / `ht-read` on `<body>`** — and `ht-tour` carries this tour's restyling of the SHARED coach furniture (caption moved to the bottom on a plate, smaller text, pips reordered above it). The pitch tutorial inherited all of it and drew its caption, which is authored at the TOP, **down over the build tag for the rest of the session**. All classes are cleared now, and `.ht-done` / `.ht-badge` no longer hang off `body.ht-tour` (they would have rendered unstyled once it was removed).
  - Asserted so neither can come back: after the tour, `.ht-done` is not shown, `#game` IS shown, **no `ht-*` classes remain on `<body>`**, and the pitch caption's y is in the top half. Screenshot `first-07-pitch-tutorial-pops.png`.

New `styled()` waits for `#settings` to compute `display:none`. - **⚠️ THE HERO STEP COULD NOT BE COMPLETED ON THE REAL PAGE — the carousel was never frozen there (agent `hub-tour-lab`). Fixed + verified; pushed the four earlier commits at the user's request.** User: "see why putting the card at the hero dosnt go to the next step toturial. and when pressing the equipped best should equipe best and wait 2 seconds before exiting the toturial".
  - **ROOT CAUSE, and it is a lesson about the lab:** `startCarouselAuto()` spins the coverflow every **2.6s** on the real page. The LAB's sandbox filters that interval out — so **the bench froze the carousel and the shipped tour did not**, and the bug was invisible to every green run. Measured live at the hero step: the front card was a **LEGENDARY** with the rare tucked behind it at 90px vs the front card's 141px. The front card is the only one anybody grabs (it is on top and takes the touch), so the obvious drag produced `setHeroSkinByRarity('legendary')` → skin `sig`, not `gold` → `done` stayed false forever.
  - **Three fixes:** (1) new seam `__hubPrefs.freezeCarousel()` / `thawCarousel()` → `stopCarouselAuto()` / `startCarouselAuto()`, raised for any tour containing a drag step; (2) the hero step's new `onEnter` **centres the rare** via the carousel's own tap-to-centre click handler, so the obvious drag IS the taught one; (3) a `fix` line — dropping a common/epic/legendary on the hero now says **«זה לא הנדיר — קח את הקלף הכחול»** at once (rare borders are blue, `#4ea0ff`) instead of leaving the kid dragging at a lesson that looks broken.
  - **«הכי טוב» now holds 2s before the tour exits** (`dwell: 2` + a `done2` caption «שלושת החזקים מצוידים!»). It is the LAST step and the exit restores the player's own loadout, so without the hold the three cards appeared and vanished in the same breath. Measured: 2.4s from tap to exit.
  - **A test trap for anyone driving the carousel:** you cannot lift a card that is BEHIND the front one — the front card takes the touch. An earlier version of the wrong-card check dragged from a side card's coordinates and silently moved the RARE instead, so it passed the lesson and failed the test. Centre the card first (`el.click()`), then drag `.cf-center`.
  - Verified: `_hub-firstrun-verify.mjs` ALL PASS — now asserts the rare is the front card, that it is STILL there 3.2s later (one full rotation), that a wrong card gets corrected and advances nothing, and that «הכי טוב» fills all three and holds ≥2s. `_hub-tour-verify.mjs` ALL PASS.

It is now in **`#settings > .settings-card`**, absolutely positioned at its top-left (`left:10 top:10, 26x26`), above the `🔊 עוצמת אפקטים` row. Reached the way a player reaches it: lobby → ⚙.

A replay **does NOT hand the floor to the pitch tutorial** the way the first-run launch does — `resumeAfter` is only set by `begin()`, so a player who asked for the lesson ends up back on the lobby. Ignores the first-run flags, so it works forever.

Measured: the `?` reported `(18,7,30)` mid-reflow and `(43,18,28)` once settled — so a tap aimed at the first reading MISSED, and "tapping the ? does nothing" was a test artifact while the button was fine. `_hub-firstrun-verify.mjs` now has `settled(sel)` (poll until the rect stops moving) and taps only after that. **Also fixed two of my own vacuous assertions**: an off-screen element reports a zero rect, and `0 < 90` is true, so corner/size checks passed while the hub was not even the visible screen — they now require a real rect.

Screenshots `first-05-question-mark-in-settings.png` + `first-06-question-mark-replay.png`, inspected.

Two answers he gave when asked: **lobby tour FIRST, then the pitch tutorial**, and **nothing the lesson does may stick**.

Locks were taken on client.js and index.html before editing.
  - **`tuMaybeAutoStart()` asks `HubTour.pending()` FIRST and hands over**, and the tour calls back when it finishes *or* is skipped → level 1 יסודות starts then. Before this a brand-new player went straight onto a pitch on the server's `welcome` and never saw the lobby. **Level 4 «מרכז» stays parked (`offered:false`, your `19fb0f0`) — this tour does not go through the level table at all**, so nothing of that decision was reversed.
  - **NOTHING IS SAVED — `window.__hubPrefs` in client.js** (myLoadout/myCosmetic/tuHub are module-private): `begin()` raises the existing `tuHub` sandbox flag + snapshots; `end()` restores both, lowers the flag, re-syncs the server; `cosmetic()` reads the live hero. **`saveLoadout` gained the `tuHub` guard `saveCosmetic` already had** — without it one tap of «הכי טוב» writes the lesson's loadout to localStorage AND postPrefs() it to the app, which saves under the player's PHONE NUMBER.
  - **⚠️ `emptySlots()` — the card half is VACUOUS without it.** `myLoadout` is null for anyone who never arranged their powers and `effectiveLoadout()` reads that as "auto-fill the top three": measured on the real page, all 3 slots arrive FULL, "drag a card into a slot" is already satisfied, «הכי טוב» has nothing to do, and the card half ran itself out in ~1s. The tour clears the slots in memory for its duration (restored by `end()`). It is also what makes the three slot explanations legible.
  - **Empty album → the 13-step legend only** (no cards to drag; the wardrobe is card-gated at 7 distinct cards per hero). Counted off the DOM.
  - **⚠️ THREE FAULTS THAT ONLY THE REAL PAGE COULD SHOW** (the lab always injects 7 cards and blocks all writes): (1) **`hubReady()` waited for a `.cf-card`, so for an empty album the tour silently never started** — now waits for hub furniture + a bounded 2s carousel grace, so a slow render cannot quietly downgrade a player who HAS cards; (2) **«דלג ✕» sat on top of the ⚙ it points at** (both want the top-inline-end corner, and ⚙ is step 1) — moved to the other corner; (3) my own assertion compared a filter against `'none'` and so **PASSED on an undimmed lobby** — `.hub-tu-gate .hub > *` has `transition: filter .18s`, so measuring at tour start reads `grayscale(0) brightness(1)`, a no-op that is not `'none'`. Dimming is asserted numerically now, after the transition.

Asserts the auto-launch, that level 1 is held back, that skip AND finish both hand over to the pitch tutorial, that a second load does not ask again, that real `localStorage` prefs are **byte-identical before and after**, and that the loadout + hero are restored. **ALL PASS.** `_hub-tour-verify.mjs` (the lab): ALL PASS.

The page therefore runs the real markup, real `style.css`, real `client.js` and **the real drag handlers** — nothing is redrawn, unlike the shipped `#tu-mock`. **Regenerate after any hub change** (every injection anchor is asserted, so it exits 1 rather than silently producing a lesson-less page). Deliberately not an `srcdoc`/`blob:` iframe: those report `about:srcdoc` for `location`, and client.js reads `location.hostname` (DEV_LOCAL) and builds its WebSocket URL out of `location`.
  - **Files (all new):** `scripts/make-hub-tour.mjs`, `public/_hub-tour-sandbox.js`, `public/_hub-tour.js`, `public/_hub-tour.css`, `public/_hub-tour.html` (generated), `_hub-tour-verify.mjs`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-hub-tour-lab-design.md`.
  - **The sandbox works only through seams the app already owns:** `SALTIZ_CARDS` (7 dummies), `SALTIZ_LOADOUT=[null,null,null]` (**without this `effectiveLoadout()` auto-fills the top three and lesson 1 is already done on arrival** — one of the four bugs that sank the live-hub tour), `SALTIZ_COSMETIC='striker:base'`, `Storage.prototype.setItem` records+drops, `WebSocket.prototype.send` drops `setLoadout`/`setCosmetic`, the 2600ms carousel auto-rotate is filtered out, and the tutorial done-keys are seeded so neither the first-run tutorial nor «מרכז» auto-launches over the lesson.
  - **Completion latches on the BLOCKED writes** (`setSlotCard`→`pikme-loadout`, `setHeroSkinByRarity`→`pikme_cosmetic`), so the lesson needs no socket and no module-private state, and the same record proves nothing escaped.
  - **Three findings from reading the shipped hub, worth keeping:** (1) a card can only be grabbed by **lifting it UP** — `dy < -16` and mostly vertical, else it reads as a carousel swipe (`client.js` ~1596) — and both drop targets sit BELOW the carousel, so every drag is up-then-over, which is what the hand mimes; (2) **`RARITY_SKIN.common === 'base'` and the hero already IS base**, so dropping a COMMON on the hero changes nothing on screen — the rare is the only card with a visible payoff, which is exactly why the user asked for one; (3) **both ends of a drag must be `.tu-live`**: `slotUnder()` uses `elementFromPoint`, which ignores `pointer-events: none`, so a gate that lights only the carousel swallows every drop.
  - **Four faults found by SCREENSHOT after the assertions were already green** (all fixed, all now asserted): the shipped `.veiled` radial spotlight computed to **r=255px** on an 844x390 screen and washed the dim off most of the lobby (dropped — the gate already dims per box, with hard edges); `.hub-tu-gate .hub > *` **cannot reach the stadium backdrop**, which is painted behind `#home` and is not a `.hub` child, so the lesson ran on a fully bright lobby while every per-element check passed (fixed with a scrim inside `.hub` at z-index 1 — above `.hub-pitch`, below every interactive box); the caption moved to the bottom put its **live pip on the «25JUL-v5» build tag** (pips reordered above the text); and the hero's `.lab-drop` dashed box was a **234x246 frame drawn across the power slots** (now a glow, at a specificity that outranks `.tu-live { filter: none }`).
  - **Two traps for whoever works this next:** `.hub-cards .cf-card` is declared TWICE in `style.css` (23x31 at ~1138, then **150x205 at ~2358, which wins**) — the hub's front card is genuinely huge, so reading the first rule mis-diagnoses a correct layout as broken. And `.tu-live { z-index: 3 }` is wrong for `#power-slots` (authored at 6): it would push the slots BEHIND the hero's button box, so the lab lifts lit targets to 7. **z-index only, never `position`** — that is what sent the last tour's ⚽ off-screen.
  - **ROUND 2 (same agent).** User: "make the hand go a bit higher. then show the equipped best." + "make another tutorial about the different element in this screen… darker except the elements ill describe in order and write what each does" with a 13-item list.
    - **Hand higher:** `LIFT` 34 → **48**, and the gesture is now two beats with a hold between them — pull straight UP, hold, then swipe across at that height, then set down. Keyframes: `0.16 (0,-48)` · `0.32 (0,-48)` · `0.56 (102,-48)` · `0.74 (164,-16)`. **The order is the mechanic:** one diagonal sweep is exactly what the carousel classifies as a SWIPE, so the old shape taught a kid to spin the deck.
    - **Lesson 3 «הכי טוב»** (`#select-best-btn`) — one tap fills all three slots. Taught LAST on purpose: first, it would fill every slot before the kid knew what a slot was and lesson 1 would have nothing empty to drop into.
    - **Tour 2, the element legend — `?tour=elements`** (or the «סיור במסך ›» button on the card finale). All 13 in the user's order, each lit while everything else is dark, with a name + one line on what it does. **Nothing navigates: every lit element is left INERT** — a tap on ⚙ or 👥 would open that screen and abandon the tour on step 1. Advance = tap anywhere (catcher at z-index 39, under `#tutorial`/the exit) or «הבא ›».
    - **Lighting ONE element out of a filter gate needs TWO classes.** A parent's filter cannot be undone by a child and the gate dims per `.hub > *`, so lighting `#training-btn` (inside `#play-strip`) = un-dim the strip (`.lab-show`) + dim its other children individually (`.lab-dim`).
    - **THE HAND-OFF BUG, worth knowing for any tour that chains into another:** `finish()` runs INSIDE a tick that has already queued its successor, so starting tour 2 immediately left tour 1's loop alive — two loops on the same globals, double `stepT`, and both able to consume one `done()` and skip a step. Every tick now carries a run token. Symptom was visual, not logical: **the second tour ran on a fully bright lobby while every per-element assertion passed.** Now asserted four ways (gate class, scrim, a dimmed non-target, and 1.0s of wall clock ⇒ ~1.0s of `stepT`).
    - **Two more screenshot-only faults:** the hand (54px) centred on ⚙ **hid the very element being described** — tap steps now anchor the hand just outside the target (below, or above when there is no room), asserted for all 13 as "the hand's box does not contain the target's centre"; and the element tour's second line must never be swapped for the idle nudge, because that line IS the lesson.
  - **ROUND 3 — THE ORDER (user):** "make the home tour first, then the cards pull, first pull a hero custom, then explain each slot, then pull a card to slot, then show the select best". Now **one run of 19 steps**: 1-13 home legend (READ) · 14 rare→hero (DO) · 15-17 what each power slot does (READ) · 18 card→slot (DO) · 19 «הכי טוב» (DO). `?tour=home` / `?tour=cards` run either half alone.
    - **The three slots are explained while still EMPTY** — the only time each shows its own ⚡/🏃/🛡️ glyph instead of a card. Copy is the GAME'S OWN (`SLOT_META` in client.js), shortened; inventing new wording would be a second, drifting description of a buff the server calculates.
    - **The tap-catcher is now PER STEP, not per tour** — one run mixes read and do steps, and a catcher left up over a drag step eats the gesture.
    - **⚠️ A "read-only" step was still TAPPABLE, and fixing it needs `!important`:** the gate's `pointer-events: none` lands on the `.hub > *` box and children inherit it — except **`#power-slots .pslot-item { pointer-events: auto }` (`style.css:2298`)**, which being an ID rule outranks any class chain. A tap on a slot mid-explanation would have opened the cards room and walked the kid off the tour. Read steps now set `body.lab-inert` → `.hub, .hub * { pointer-events: none !important }`. **Found by assertion, not by eye.**
    - **`icon-system.js` REPLACES emoji in the DOM with pixel sprites**, so `textContent` of a caption written as «⚡ בעיטה» comes back as «בעיטה». Assertions on caption text must use `includes`, not equality. (The sprite is wanted — it is the game's own look.)
    - **The caption-clearance assertion was too strict** and failed on a 9px graze: the hero's button box is 246px tall and its bottom edge laps into the caption while the 210px character canvas centred in it is nowhere near. Now: the caption must miss the target's CENTRE and cover ≤25% of its height.
  - **Verified:** `node _hub-tour-verify.mjs` → **ALL PASS** in real Chrome over CDP with real touch — both drags performed, a real slot really filled, the hero really went `base → gold` (asserted on BOTH the blocked `pikme_cosmetic` write and the intercepted `setCosmetic('striker:gold')`), zero `localStorage` writes, zero app-bridge post, no console errors. Four screenshots in `_tu-shots/lab-*.png`, all four inspected. `test-tutorial.mjs`: ALL PASS.

- **Tutorial: the dark screen now appears ONLY on the four steps that introduce a new button, and goes on the first press; a tutorial goal gets the goal badge and moves on after 1s (agent `tu-veil-goal`).   - **The veil rule, one line:** it paints only while a step is handing over a control the kid has NEVER had, and it goes on their first press of it, for the rest of the step. Four veils in the whole tutorial. Before this it was up on every step of every level.
  - **New pure helper `introducesFor(l, n)`** (`shared/tutorial.js`, beside `tuHasControl`) → the controls this step is the FIRST IN THE WHOLE CURRICULUM to enable. First-appearance ACROSS the levels, not a diff against the previous step in the same level: the within-level diff re-creates the reported bug twice over (L2 step 1 lists move+aim, taught a level earlier; L3's fly step lists 💣, taught in L2). Levels only unlock in order, so "an earlier level taught it" is a fact. Output is exactly the user's four buttons — L1/`move`:move · L1/`charge`:aim · L2/`bomb`:bomb · L2/`wall`:wall. A HUB level is guarded to return nothing.
  - **Client:** `tuIntroUsed`, a per-step one-way latch reset in `tuEnter` and on every advance, set in **`tuGate`** off the GATED input (a tap on a button this step never taught is already zeroed, so it can never clear the veil) — stick past `TU_STICK_DEAD` 0.15 · `hold/fire/aimed` · `special` · `build/buildHold`. `tuRenderOverlay` toggles `.veiled` instead of the old always-on paint. **`.tutorial.no-spot` is gone** (client + CSS): it was the flat dim for steps pointing at the GRASS, and those introduce nothing. **The hand, the caption, the second line and the pips are untouched and still run on every step.**
  - **LEVEL 4 (the hub tour) KEEPS ITS VEIL** — `tuIsHub` exemption in that one toggle. The hub tour renders through the same `tuRenderOverlay`, so the naive change would have silently un-dimmed level 4, whose every step points at furniture the kid has never seen. Nothing about the hub was reported.
  - **The goal badge is BACK for tutorials** (`processSnapshotSounds`): the `if (!tutorial)` that suppressed `confettiBurst` + `triggerCelebration` is gone. The reason it was suppressed is real — the comic word draws at `H*0.40` with a starburst reaching `S*0.30`, and `.tu-caption` sits at `top:64px`, so on a landscape phone they overlap — so the collision is ANSWERED, not avoided: new `.tutorial.celebrating .tu-caption` (opacity+visibility) steps the coach's words aside for the beat the word is up, and the word is cut to `TU_CELEB_SEC` 0.8s via a new optional `over` argument on `triggerCelebration(kind, over)`. During a goal beat the standing caption is an instruction the kid has just obeyed, so nothing is lost. The class is toggled in `tuTick` ABOVE the freeze return (the whole beat happens inside the freeze) and cleared in `tuExit`/`tuEnter`/`tuHubEnter` — `tuTick` stops running when a level ends on a goal, so without that the hub tour would inherit an invisible caption.
  - **The 1s advance is a CLAMP in the room, not a new timer** (`updateTutorial`, `server.js`): `st.resetTimer = Math.min(st.resetTimer, TU_GOAL_HOLD)` (1.0s, new export in `shared/tutorial.js`) in the existing `pendingReset` branch. **Clamped, never zeroed:** the client's `scored` latch — the only thing that completes a goal step — is the `resetTimer` 0→positive edge with `lastGoal` set, and that edge went out in the snapshot for the tick BEFORE this one, so shortening the window cannot erase it (and 60 more snapshots carry it). The UN-FREEZE is still `step()`'s own `resetTimer <= 0` branch, and the coach still waits for `resetTimer === 0` before advancing, so the next stage is applied to a body that can move. Applies to every scoring step (L1 `goal`/`super`, L2 `ballshot`) because it is keyed to the goal, not to a step.

- **Tutorial L1: the FULL shot is now step 1 and must HIT; the quick shot is step 2 and is just 3 taps (agent `tu-l1-shots`). Code written, UNVERIFIED (no tests run — user's standing "you do not test"), uncommitted — parent session commits + restarts `:3012`.** User, from his phone: "in train 1, make the first do a full shoot, which must hits the enemy. then the quick shoot which dosnt need to hit the enemy (just 3 taps on the shoot)".
  - **New L1 order: `move → charge → shoot → goal → super`** (ids unchanged, places swapped — each id is still named for the gesture it teaches, so nothing outside the table had to be renamed).
    - `charge` (step 2 of the level): hold to full, release, and it must LAND on the dummy. New flag **`chargedHit`** — the old `chargedShot` was a release at full charge and never asked where the bullet went, so «ירייה חזקה!» could congratulate a shot into the touchline. New correction **`underHeld`** («החזק יותר — עד שהטבעת מלאה»), the mirror of the tap step's over-hold line, sampled at the RELEASE because letting go early is only knowable once you have let go.
    - `shoot` (step 3): **three quick releases, no hit at all** — a kid firing three taps into empty grass passes, by design (the aim was taught by the step before it). Completion is a COUNT: new tally **`quickShots`** + new step field **`needs: 3`**.
  - **How a count fits the pure layer** (`shared/tutorial.js`): `isStepDone` gains one branch — `if (s.needs) return (ctx[s.done] || 0) >= s.needs` — the eight boolean latches never enter it. `whenLatched(s, ctx)` (new, exported) makes `cap2`/`when` fire on the FULL count instead of on truthiness, or «3 מהירות!» would replace the counter on tap one.
  - **Visible tap progress with no new overlay element:** `{k}`/`{t}` tokens in a caption or second line, filled by `captionFor`/`subFor`. The tap step's cap is «הקש! {k}/{t}» → «הקש! 2/3». It rides the existing `.tu-cap`, and because the text changes on each tap the caption's pop re-runs — one free beat per tap.
  - **Client latches (`public/client.js`):** `releaseShot` now stamps `tuFullShotAt` at/above `FULL_CHARGE` (mirroring `tuQuickShotAt` under `QUICK_CHARGE`; each release clears the other's stamp), bumps `tuEv.quickShots` on every sub-`QUICK_CHARGE` release, and latches `underHeld` for a step that asks for it. The projectile-impact branch in `processSnapshotSounds` latches `chargedHit` the same way it already did `quickHit`. `TU_QUICK_HIT_MS` → **`TU_SHOT_HIT_MS`** (2000): `PROJECTILE.ttl` is 1.3s whatever the charge, only the distance differs, so one window serves both stamps.
  - **Stages did NOT move, because L1's two shooting stages are byte-identical** (parked ball, still dummy, loitering keeper, neither pins `me`) — written down in the table so the index-matching rule is not rediscovered. Geometry checked instead of assumed: neither stage teleports the kid, so they shoot from inside `TU_RING` (r 80) = **383–515px** from `TU_DUMMY`. A full-charge bullet carries ~936px (reaches from anywhere in the ring); a quick tap only ~410px, i.e. **from the ring's far side a tap could not land a hit at all — which is precisely why the tap step must not require one.** The swap makes the geometry safer than it was.

## 2026-07-27

- **HUB TOUR — tutorial level 4 «מרכז» (agent `hub-tour`). BUILT, VERIFIED, merged to main, NOT pushed by me.** User: "you collaborate with the other agent to build the tutorial... report back" -> "ask how to chunk the HUB tour (quick play/friends/trophies/deck/slots/heroes)" -> chose a LEVEL in the picker + demo cards + auto-launch + drag-to-equip -> mid-build: "maybe just show a mock lobby?" -> chose mock for the lessons, real ⚽ to finish.
  - **Design (AMENDED after the build): [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-hub-tour-level4-design.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-hub-tour-level4-design.md).** Read its ⚠️ box FIRST — the body still describes the demo-album design that was reversed. Plan: [`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-hub-tour-level4.md`](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-hub-tour-level4.md).
  - **What shipped:** level 4 `mercaz` (🏠, `TU_LEVELS` index 3). Six steps — 🏆 גביעים (dwell) · 🃏 קלפים · ⚡ כוחות (drag) · 🦸 גיבור · 👥 חברים on a **mock lobby the tutorial draws itself** (`tuMockBuild`), then ⚽ on the **real hub**, whose tap starts an actual match. Auto-launches on the first hub visit, skippable (`fbTuHubSkipped`), replayable from 🎓 forever.
  - **`where: 'hub'` on a level = no room, no socket, no stages. `server.js` IS NOT TOUCHED.** `stageAt`/`foeKeys` now tolerate a stageless level; the server's tutorial hooks already guarded `if (!stage) return`, so a client asking for level 4 gets an empty pitch, not a crash.
  - **`tuUnlocked` exemption:** a hub level gates on level 1 ALONE, not the sequential chain — otherwise auto-launch-on-first-hub-visit could never fire.
  - **WHY THE MOCK (please don't re-litigate):** it first ran on the LIVE hub and a browser found four bugs sharing one root — the wardrobe is an OVERLAY not a screen swap; the carousel AUTO-ROTATES and completed a step with zero input; `effectiveLoadout()` auto-fills the top 3 so the drag step was done on arrival; and **localhost injects a sample album** (`DEV_LOCAL`), which hides the empty-album path. **Test on the LAN IP, never localhost.**
  - **⚠️ THREE TRAPS FOR ANY FUTURE HUB-OVERLAY WORK:**
    1. **`#tutorial` lives INSIDE `#game`**, which is `display:none` on the hub — hand/caption/pips render into a hidden subtree while `textContent` assertions pass happily. It is re-homed onto `<body>` for the level and restored on exit.
    2. **Never set `position` on a hub box.** The BAKED LOBBY LAYOUT positions them absolutely; a gate rule with `position: relative` threw the finale's ⚽ to x=1457 in an 844px viewport, off-screen. Use z-index alone.
    3. **Touch pointers keep IMPLICIT CAPTURE** — a drag onto a slot is delivered to the CARD, so the slot never hears the drop. Hit-test the release with `elementFromPoint` (what `bindSlotDrag` always did).
  - **The tutorial writes NOTHING:** guards on `setSlotCard`/`swapSlots`/`saveCosmetic`, because `saveLoadout`/`saveCosmetic` -> `postPrefs()` -> the app persists prefs **under the player's phone number**. Asserted: no `localStorage` write, no app-bridge post.
  - **Files taken:** `shared/tutorial.js`, `public/client.js`, `public/style.css`, `test-tutorial.mjs`, new `_tu-hub-verify.mjs`. NOT `server.js`, NOT `public/index.html` (skip button and mock are built in JS).
  - **Verified:** `node _tu-hub-verify.mjs` = **48/48** in real Chrome with real touch, a screenshot per step, screenshots INSPECTED (two layout faults were found that way after the checks were already green). `test-tutorial.mjs` ALL PASS; `test-party`/`test-field`/`test-back-nav`/`test-quick-chat` pass. Built in a worktree, rebased onto `70fc067` (resolving the `TU3_HIDE`->`TU3_FIND` rename), and re-verified 48/48 AFTER the rebase.

- **Tutorial onboarding — three scripted pitch levels for kids (agent `tutorial-coach`). SHIPPED + PUSHED.** User: "ok now i want to do a tutorial onboarding for the football game... this is aim for kids so the toturial should be very intiutive and simple. also see what brawlstars and fortnight are doing" -> "do the simplest way and such that can alwys replay the toturial" -> "yes lets build, do this in local host first" -> "now add the second, using bombs building walls, shooting at the ball. detach ball from enemy etc." -> "push for each toturial block" -> "you should also explain that holding the aim the longer make the shoot and kick stronger" -> a gesture-and-wording pass.
  - **Design + rationale: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-tutorial-onboarding-design.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-tutorial-onboarding-design.md).** Carries the Brawl Stars / Epic research, the level tables, and the alternatives rejected and why.
  - **Commits (all PUSHED):** `a31a46d` level 1 · `4baab4d` level 2 + the charge step + the level picker · `686a72f` level 3 first cut + minDwell + tap-vs-drag · `3c28588` the gesture/wording pass.
  - **What I built:** L1 יסודות (move · tap · hold · kick · super) · L2 קרב (shoot-the-ball · bomb · wall · strip) · L3 טריקים first cut. Silent coach: spotlight + a MIMED hand + 1-2 Hebrew words, never pauses, no clock, no fail state. Level 1 auto-launches once on a first run with no skip; every level is replayable forever from `אימון → 🎓 איך משחקים?`. Celebration-only reward (`c86fa82` "practice pays nothing" holds).
  - **A LEVEL IS DATA.** `shared/tutorial.js` = a `steps` table + a declarative `stages` table that `server.js` interprets (`applyTuStage`/`updateTutorial`/`tutorialPost`). Adding a level is a data change. Keep that module PURE (no DOM/sockets/timers) — that is what makes the rules unit-testable.
  - **`minDwell`** holds a COMPLETED step open N more seconds. Some lessons only land in the seconds after the act (the wall standing there taking fire). `isStepDone` = achieved; `advance()` = achieved AND seen.
  - **Two bugs only the BROWSER caught, both invisible to DOM assertions:** (1) `.tu-hand` positioned itself with a `transform: translate(...)` while its keyframes animate `scale` — CSS applies scale BEFORE transform, so the position vector got scaled and the hand rendered ~110px off a right-hand button, worse while nudging. Position now lives in left/top. On a screen where every other step's hand moves, a still hand reads as "nothing here". - **LEVEL 3 REWORKED: step 1 is now FIND-the-hidden-bot, step 2 flies off a pre-built steel wall (agent `tu-coach-css`).** User: "in the stage 3 of toturial, make the first step the bot hiding and you should find the bot, when you see it it says hide in bush or somthing and go to the next step. in the second step put a steal wall behind the player and tell him to just tap the bomb and aim in the direction he wants to fly."
  - **Files taken: `shared/tutorial.js`, `public/client.js`, `test-tutorial.mjs`.** No `server.js` change was needed — `fieldFor(l)` and the declarative stage table already carry all of it. Committed on top of `3c28588`, so none of the gesture-pass work is in my commit.
  - **Step 1 `hide` → `find`.** The watcher is now planted INSIDE the bush (`TU3_FIND.foe` = the bush centre) and the kid starts ~610px away, so it is genuinely invisible (`BUSH_REVEAL_DIST` 110). Caption «איפה הוא?» → on the sighting it swaps to «הוא היה בשיח!» + «גם אתה יכול להתחבא שם». **The lesson is taught from the other side of the bush**: a kid who has just failed to see somebody works out for themselves that the same bush hides them; being told to go stand in the green teaches only that the coach knows a rule. No `marker` — a cue pointing at the bush would answer the question. `minDwell: 2.5` so both lines get read.
  - **New completion flag `foundFoe`** (replaces `hidden`, which no step used any more). Latched client-side off **`bushRevealAlpha(foe) >= 0.6`** — the renderer's own stealth value, so what completes the step is literally what the kid can see, not a distance that might disagree with the sprite. Needs the snapshot player object, so `tuFoePos` was split into `tuFoeSnap` (object) + `tuFoePos` (x/y wrapper).
  - **New `sub2` + `subFor(l,n,ctx)`** in `shared/tutorial.js`: the second line now swaps on the step's `when` flag exactly like `cap2` does. That swap IS the find step's teaching device. Client's second-line priority is now nudge → `sub2` → `sub`.
  - **Step 2 `fly`: the steel wall is now part of the pitch** (`TU3_WALL`, in `TU3_FIELD.hardWalls`) at `cx 540`, 84px behind the kid's spawn — inside `BOMB_WALL_DIST` (150) so it cannons (×1.24) but not close enough to stand in. **🧱 is no longer in that step's controls at all**: building is level 2's lesson and charging it as rent on level 3's turned one trick into a two-part errand. Caption «תעוף!» / sub «כוון לאן שתרצה לעוף · הקש 💣 · ואל תזוז».
  - **Why "aim then tap" is exact, not a simplification:** a tap-planted bomb sits under your feet, and `explode()` has no radial direction to throw you in when you are dead-centre (`rd > 6` fails), so **it uses your aim**. `applyTuStage` already points every stage's aim at +x, so a kid who taps 💣 and touches nothing else flies right into the open pitch first try. The one failure mode is aiming INTO the steel — `jumpBlocked` cancels the launch outright — so that step's nudge is «כוון לצד הפתוח — לא לקיר», about direction rather than about the button.
  - **`TU3_FIELD`/`TU3_BUSH`/`TU3_WALL` moved DOWN** into the level-3 geometry section, below `MID`. `TU3_WALL` reads `MID` inside an object initializer and the old position was above `MID`'s declaration — that is the exact TDZ shape that killed the whole of `client.js` once already (see the lesson higher up this log).

Someone should run `node test-tutorial.mjs` before this is pushed. `_tu-verify.mjs` needed no change (it never reaches level 3).

Scenery block moved above the spawn spots so they can read it without a TDZ. Centre leaves a 150px margin on the short axis — the reveal fade starts 110px out, so a watcher near the rim would be half-visible from outside the leaves and there would be nothing to find.

- **New players start at 50% volume for both music and SFX (agent `corner-style`).** User: "i want the voulme at the start of the game for new player when they start to be at 50% for both songs and sounds."
  - **File taken:** `public/client.js` only (2 literals + comment, orchestration lock held/released). No markup change — the sliders in `index.html` carry no `value` attribute, they take their position from these variables.
  - `soundVol` 0.72 → **0.5**, `musicUserVol` 0.6 → **0.5** (`public/client.js` ~line 283/285). These are STARTING values only: the `pikme-soundvol` / `pikme-musicvol` reads a few lines below still win, so no existing player's own setting is rewritten.
  - **Verified in real Chrome (CDP, :3063/:9363, clear of other agents' ports), 6/6:** a fresh browser profile (genuinely empty localStorage — nothing stored) opens BOTH sliders at 0.5; a player with stored 0.15 SFX / 0.9 music reloads and keeps exactly those, not 50%; moving a slider still persists (0.35 written through). `node --check` clean; no test pins the old defaults.

- **Arena builder corners: auto-built + styled per corner, not per session (agent `corner-style`).** User: "in the arena builder there is a bug with the corner build. 1. the corners should be built automatically not from user 2. the corner choice could be done from user while selecting the corner."
  - **What was wrong:** corner GEOMETRY was already automatic (`wallJoints`/`jointPolygon` derive a hull where walls meet), but the LOOK was one global flag — `#b-joint` flipped `fbJointStyle` in `localStorage['pikme-joint-style']` for every corner of every field, and the author had to choose it up front, before drawing the walls it applied to.
  - **Files taken:** `public/client.js` (13 hunks, orchestration lock held), `shared/arena.js` (1 line), new `shared/joint-style.js` + `test-joint-style.mjs`. Left `public/index.html` / `public/style.css` alone — the `⬛ פינה` button was reused, no markup change.
  - **Now:** every corner auto-styles from its own angle — AUTO rounds exactly the corners whose mitre `jointPolygon` already refuses (1/sin(θ/2) > MITER 3.0, i.e. sharper than ~38.9°) and squares the rest, so there is ONE threshold, not two. Tap a corner (✋ move tool, or no tool) → it highlights → `פינה` cycles THAT corner auto → square → round → auto. Overrides live on the field (`fbField.joints`, keyed `"x:y"`), so they travel with a saved field, into the match (`buildArenaFromField` carries `joints`), and through undo. Field shape is **version 4**; v1/v3 fields and the presets have no `joints` = every corner AUTO = exactly how they looked before. `'pikme-joint-style'` is dropped from `PREF_KEYS`; stale values are ignored. Orphaned overrides are pruned on save so a deleted corner cannot bequeath its style to a new corner at the same junction.
  - **Verified:** `test-joint-style.mjs` 37 assertions (new); real Chrome via CDP on :3061/:9361 (clear of the other agent's :3055/:9333) drove the actual builder — drew two walls into an acute wedge, and 19/19 checks passed: corner appears with no user action, AUTO renders it as a disc, tap selects + highlights, button reports AUTO (not a fake override), press → square override persisted on the field, → round, → back to AUTO leaving NO key behind, saves as version 4, and undo reverts a restyle. Screenshot inspected. Suite: `test-field`, `test-behavior`, `test-fragile`, `test-cover`, `test-fog`, `test-autoaim`, `test-arena-plan`, `test-vs-consistency` all pass.
  - **The browser caught a bug the unit tests could not:** `pointer-events` was set at render time only when the move tool was already active, so a corner drawn with the wall tool stayed untappable until the next repaint. Joints are now always tappable and the pointerdown decides what a tap means (drawing over a corner still draws — a joint is not a `.bel`).
  - **Status: done, committed locally, NOT pushed** (no push was asked for).

- **Team-page chat: rail → hovering half-screen sheet + bottom-left bubble icon (agent `chat-sheet`), revision of the team-first party redesign below (shipped ~an hour earlier).** User: "in the play with friends I want the chat menu to pop up over / hovering the my team page, half screen. and make a small chat icon bubble in the bottom left instead, so when a player clicks it it opens the chat hover, and when click the room it goes back down."

Removed `#party`'s permanent right-hand rail (`.party-chat-rail` + the `--party-rail-w` width reservation on `#party .subpage`) — roster/games get full width back. New `#party-chat-bubble` (round, bottom-left, own safe-area inset, real `saltiz-icon si-chat` art — the SAME glyph `#chat-btn` uses for in-match quick chat) toggles new `#party-chat-sheet`, which slides up from the bottom edge to ~50% viewport height via a CSS transform transition. `#party-chat-log`/`#party-chat` (existing markup/render functions, untouched) moved inside it.
  - **Dismiss follows the existing idiom, doesn't invent one:** `partyEl`'s `isDismissBackdrop` click handler now checks `partyChatSheetIsOpen()` first — while open, a backdrop tap closes the SHEET instead of running `leaveToLobby()`; only once closed does a backdrop tap fall through to its original "leave the room" meaning. The icon's own tap toggles via `stopPropagation` (same guard `partyAddTile`'s `+` tile already uses), so it can never double as a backdrop tap. `closePartyChatSheet()` added alongside every existing `closeInviteSheet()` call (leaving the party, the non-host auto-follow into `#lobby`, the host's own click-to-start) so it never lingers open across a screen swap.
  - **Small unread-hint dot** (`.pchat-bubble-dot`, gold, pulses with the existing `fmtPulse` keyframe) reuses the same `member.chat`/`chat.at` state `notePartyChatHistory` already tracks — just one new "sheet last opened at" timestamp, no new tracking system.
  - **Measured the 390px-landscape budget over CDP, didn't assume it fit:** at a flat 844×390 viewport, a half-height sheet (195px) fits the composer (74px) and a 2-message log (both rows fully visible) comfortably. Trimmed a few px of pure whitespace (composer separator, log row padding/gap — never font-size) after measuring. Under a synthetic 44px `Emulation.setSafeAreaInsetsOverride` stress test (uniform on all 4 sides — more aggressive than a real device's landscape bottom inset, which is typically much smaller than that), the log's own budget drops to ~63px: one message stays fully visible, the other shows roughly a third of itself rather than being fully readable. Flagged rather than silently accepted; the further trade to fully fix the worst case would be shrinking the log row itself (e.g. dropping the separate name line and inlining "Name: message" on one line), which starts to touch the log's existing render output rather than just its CSS chrome, so left as-is pending the user's call.
  - **Verified over CDP at 844×390** (own tab via `/json/new` on the existing `:9333` debugger, dedicated test server on `:3910`, not `:3012`/`:3010`; real `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` pointerdown+mouseup sequences, not `.click()`, since the backdrop-dismiss guard depends on a real pointerdown preceding the click): rail gone + full-width roster/games confirmed both by DOM (`#party-chat-rail` absent) and by measured `getBoundingClientRect`; bubble icon's computed `background-image` resolves to the real sprite sheet URL; sheet opens to exactly half height (195/390) hovering over the still-visible team page; two real chat sends (server-throttled 1.5s apart) both land in the log; a tap on `#party h2` (backdrop) closes the sheet AND leaves `#party`/`#home`'s visibility untouched (proving it did not also call `leaveToLobby`); re-opening then tapping the icon again toggles closed the same safe way; the safe-area override moves the icon/composer insets from 10px to 44px live. Zero console errors/exceptions/log entries across every step, both driver runs.
  - **Full suite for this surface:** `test-party.mjs`, `test-party-3v3-chat.mjs`, `test-party-invite-flow.mjs`, `test-back-nav.mjs` (the one that guards the team page's back affordance) all pass, plus `test-quick-chat.mjs`/`test-quick-messages.mjs` for adjacent coverage. `node --check` clean; boot+curl 200 on `:3910`.

Reconstructed a "HEAD + my 6 edits only" version of `client.js`, verified it byte-for-byte against both HEAD (only my changes) and the live working tree (the remainder was exactly their joint-style hunks, nothing of mine), staged only that reconstruction, then restored the full mixed file to the working tree so their uncommitted work was never touched. `public/index.html`/`public/style.css` had no such overlap. - **Tasks 8-9 of the matchmaking-countdown build (spec: `.superpowers/sdd/2026-07-27-matchmaking-countdown/`), combined into one pass: the end-to-end socket test, the full suite run, screenshots, and this handoff.** Tasks 1-7 were already committed and pushed (pure matcher, ticket queue, named bots, the three search-screen states).
  - **Three commits, local only:** `194dabc` (fix: bot roster lost its name+level between the countdown preview and the live match — found by the new test, see below), `bc36409` (Task 8: new `test-matchmaking-live.mjs`, boots its own server per `boot-test-server.mjs`, 6 sections over real sockets), and this entry + the spec status line (Task 9, docs only, commit follows).
  - **Files taken:** `server.js` (the fix only), new `test-matchmaking-live.mjs`, `AGENT_REQUEST_LOG.md`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-matchmaking-countdown-design.md`. Locks held per file, released between steps; `git status` showed nobody else's changes on any of them before each commit.
  - **A real bug the new test caught, not a test bug:** a solo `quickMatch`'s `matchStart.players` showed every bot as literally `"Bot"` with no `.level`, even though the SAME wait's countdown preview (`lobbyPayload`'s `bots` array) showed real Hebrew names with a level/XP badge — preview and the live roster silently disagreed. Root cause: `computeBotPlan()`'s generic filler bots never got a `.name` (only INVITED lobby bots did), so `fillBots()`'s `planned.name` was absent and fell back to `'Bot'`; `lobbyPayload` compensated by calling `pickBotIdentities()` a SECOND time, for display only, never writing the result back onto `room.botPlan`. `fillBots()`'s roster entry also never set `.level`/`.xp` (only the differently-scaled `botLevel`), which is what the client's `fillIntroCol` reads for the "רמה N · X XP" badge. Fixed in `194dabc`: `computeBotPlan()` now stamps `.name` on every filler slot from ONE `pickBotIdentities()` call, `lobbyPayload` reads that same name back off the plan instead of re-rolling it, and `fillBots()`'s roster entry gains `level`/`xp` via the same `displayLevelForBot`/`xpForBotLevel` conversion the preview already used.
  - **Corrected timings, measured, not the brief's:** a lone player resolves at 2000-3500ms (`MM_ALONE_MS` short-circuit + the 1500ms reveal), not the brief's ~4000ms; two humans need the full 5000ms `MM_BUDGET_QUICK_MS` ticket deadline to be grouped, not ~3000ms (at 3000ms both are still mid-widen). Same correction Tasks 4-7 already found independently below. `test-matchmaking-live.mjs` and the screenshot capture both used the corrected numbers.
  - **`test-matchmaking-live.mjs` — 6 sections, 21/21 assertions pass after the fix:** lone player short-circuits fast and reports its own band before any room exists; two same-band humans matched onto OPPOSITE teams; an L1 and an L11 never share a room (rookie faces low-level bots, veteran high-level — confirmed via the newly-wired `.level` field); a full 4-human room resolves in ~15ms, not the 10s mode budget; cancelling drops the ticket and the remaining player's `searchingCount` falls 2→1; training and private rooms still bypass matchmaking entirely (no `searching` message ever sent). Read `test-matchmaking-teams.mjs` first and did not duplicate its existing trophy-ranked-alternating-teams coverage.
  - **Full suite (watchdog script, all `test*.mjs`, 87 files): PASS=85 FAIL=1 HANG=1.** The only fail/hang are the two known PRE-EXISTING issues in the bot-difficulty area, unrelated to matchmaking — **not this work**: `test-bot-partner.mjs` (FAIL) and `test-bot-ladder.mjs` (HANG). Nothing else regressed.
  - **Screenshots at 844×390 over CDP** (own dedicated server on :3055, left the shared :3012/:3044 surfaces other agents were using untouched): SEARCHING (~1.5s: title "מחפש יריבים...", band "רמה 1", 1/4 pips lit, both away rows dashed "מחפש..."); NO PLAYERS (~3.6s, inside the corrected 2000-3500ms window: "אין שחקנים פנויים כרגע", both away slots now show real named bots with a level/XP badge); FOUND (two humans with distinct names, ~6.7s past the corrected 5000ms deadline: "נמצאו יריבים!", each side's away column shows the OTHER real human's name plus one bot filling the last slot). Checked with `getBoundingClientRect`, not just eyeballing: the search-head row's title/pips/band sit in a straight line 8px apart, zero overlap. Hebrew renders in correct RTL character order throughout (e.g. the trailing "..." lands left of "מחפש", as RTL requires) — the page's container-level CSS `direction` is `ltr` sitewide (pre-existing, not touched by Tasks 1-9); Hebrew glyphs still order themselves correctly via Unicode bidi regardless. Zero console errors/exceptions on any of the three tabs.
  - **Minor, not fixed:** `#ti-search-cancel` (centered on the true viewport center) overlaps the LEFT team column's outer border by ~6px over an 18px vertical band — the gutter between the two `.ti-col` boxes isn't quite centered on the viewport, and the cancel pill is 1-2px wider than the gutter. No text collides and nothing is illegible; a pre-existing Task 7 layout, flagging for a future pixel pass rather than touching `public/style.css` (actively edited by other agents this session).

The fix for the app is `pikme-server` `ae9e39a`: `/handle-friends/rank` — the one token-authed route that already resolves `UserInfo → phone` server-side — now also returns the 20-field career block. Purely additive; pre-existing keys untouched (`rankTier` stays a tier NAME because the app maps it onto badge art).

(2) Unreadable stats printed `0`; "0 wins" is a claim about the player, "we could not read it" is a different statement — the tiles now print an em dash and the career block prints one sentence instead of ten zeros. (3) The header read `window.SALTIZ_XP` while the body used the fetched row, so the page showed **רמה 5 · 1.2K next to a body reporting 42 matches** — the fetched row now wins so the page describes ONE snapshot.

## 2026-07-26

- **Request (23:0x): "revise the arenas, put the players position (like the one in the arena builder) in the correct place, and the ball starting position in all games starting from the middle. when a team concedes then the ball starts with one of their players."**
  - Files I took: `shared/sim.js`, `shared/field-spawns.js`, `public/client.js` (builder region), `public/index.html` (materials rail), `server.js` (match-start calls), `test-field-spawns.mjs`, new `test-kickoff-rule.mjs`.
  - **Player positions: the formation formula MOVED out of sim.js into `shared/field-spawns.js` (`formationSlot` + `defaultSpawns`).** It was private to the sim, so the builder had no way to draw it — a field with no authored slots showed an **empty pitch** in the builder while the match used invisible positions. Now both read the same function, and the builder seeds the real formation (`fbSeedSpawns`) on open / restore / load / clear-all. Verified in headless Chrome: 4 markers, 2 per team, badge « 2 נגד 2 », no console errors.
  - **Ball: one rule for every field and format, replacing the per-field authored spot.** Kickoff = loose in the exact centre; after a goal = attached to a player of the **conceding** team. `attachBall()` is the only thing that decides it, and `repositionKickoff` now calls it **unconditionally** (it used to seed the ball from the authored spot and only call attachBall `if (ballTeam)` — two places deciding one thing).
  - **The conceding side's holder is now its CENTRE-MOST player**, not `players.find(...)`, which returned whoever joined first — a 3v3/5v5 restart could be handed to a winger. 3v3 now restarts from slot 1, 5v5 from slot 2.
  - **Removed the builder's ⚽ ball tool** (button, placement branch, marker) rather than leave a control that silently does nothing. `state.ballSpawn` and `ballStart()` are gone; `field.ball` on an older save round-trips but is ignored. `normBall` stays in field-spawns.js for its own tests.
  - **Verified:** new `test-kickoff-rule.mjs` — 47 assertions, including an **end-to-end** section that drives real goals through `step()` (2v2/3v3/5v5, both directions) through the GOAL_RESET freeze and asserts who holds the ball at the restart, so a wrong `pendingBallTeam` cannot pass. Plus: mirrored halves, no two players sharing a spot, every slot in its own half, and short-handed teams degrading to centre instead of nowhere.
  - `test-field-spawns.mjs` §5 asserted the OPPOSITE (authored spot wins) and legitimately failed; its three cases are **inverted, not deleted**, so re-introducing the override fails there too. 31/31.
  - Suite: **76 pass / 2 fail / 1 hang.** Pre-existing and not mine — `test-bot-partner.mjs` (partner-level cliff + cadence ranking) and `test-bot-ladder.mjs` (hangs; multi-seed harness), both the bot agent's area.
  - ⚠️ **For the bot agent: my `public/index.html` + `public/client.js` edits were swept into your commit `30f7152`** (it is the commit that removed `data-tool="ball"`). Nothing was lost and nothing needs undoing — recorded only so the diff/message mismatch in that commit isn't a mystery later. My `shared/` files you correctly left alone are committed separately.

- **Request (22:0x): in play-with-friends — (1) add the 3v3 option, (2) add the quick chat plus FREE TEXT up to 40 chars, shown as a chat bubble on the team page, so friends can communicate.**
  - Asked one question before building: free text reverses `shared/quick-messages.js`'s stated "no free text so there is nothing to moderate" policy. He chose **private/party rooms only** — the server refuses free text anywhere else, and `FREE_TEXT_ROOMS` is the single place that policy is written down.
  - **3v3 in a party needed more than un-hiding the card.** A room's capacity is `teamSize × 2` and the format was only applied at MATCH START, so a party physically could not seat more than 4 — picking 3v3 and inviting five friends failed with "החדר מלא" and no explanation. New `partyGame` message applies the format the moment the host picks it (host-only, lobby-only), publishes it to every member, and **refuses a shrink** that would strand players instead of silently dropping them.
  - Chat: `partyChat` takes a preset id or free text; one shared sanitizer (`sanitizeFreeText`) counts **code points** so an emoji is one character, strips control/bidi characters, collapses padding, and is used by BOTH the composer's live counter and the server's validation. 1.2s per-member rate limit. The message rides on the existing lobby broadcast as `member.chat`, so no new packet type and the sender sees exactly what the room received.
  - Files: `shared/quick-messages.js`, `server.js`, `public/client.js`, `public/index.html`, `public/style.css`, new `test-party-3v3-chat.mjs` (20 assertions, boots its own server).

It is now an in-flow slot bounded to the hero's width. Verified in headless Chrome at 390×900 with a full six-player party.

- **Request (20:3x): "on my phone I get a lot of «חיבור לא יציב» — is that my internet, or the bot progression we did?"** — diagnosis only, **no code changed**.
  - **NOT the bot work, measured.** New instrument `net-load.mjs` (scratchpad) drives the REAL server over real WebSockets and measures the exact number the client's classifier reads. Snapshot rate held **59.2–59.5/s with 0.39–0.47 ms jitter and an 18 ms worst gap at 1, 4, 10, 20 AND 40 concurrent level-10 bot matches** (40 rooms = 160 bots). Zero degradation. With `bot-cost.mjs`'s 0.31 µs per bot per tick, there is no route from bot logic to that warning. (Measured on a dev Mac, not Render — but the headroom is ~40×, so a 10× slower CPU does not change the answer.)
  - **The warning is largely RTT in disguise.** `public/net-quality.js` `NET_T` gates on rtt 110/180, jitter 30/60, snapRate 48/30 and **unacked 6/12** — and `unacked = pendingInputs.length` with `INPUT_RATE = 60` (client.js:49), so **unacked ≈ 60 × RTT**: **100 ms RTT alone reads "fair", 200 ms reads "poor"**, however smooth the match is. Two of the four signals are therefore the same signal.
  - **The link to prod is genuinely spiky, but not bad.** From a desktop on good WiFi to `wss://pikme-football.onrender.com` (`net-rtt.mjs`, speaks the game's own ping): **mean 73 ms, p50 65, p95 199, jitter 35 ms** → the **jitter signal already reads FAIR from a good connection**, before a phone or cellular is involved. Localhost jitter is 0 ms, so the 35 ms is network + Render scheduling, not the game loop.
  - **So: mostly the thresholds, partly the link, not the bots.** The net-warnings handoff already says it (§4): "**Thresholds are estimates**, not measured against real player traffic — retune `NET_T`." This is that retune becoming due. Principled fix (NOT applied, awaiting the user): drop or derive `unacked` from RTT instead of counting queue depth at a fixed 60 Hz send rate, and widen `jitter`/`rtt` for a phone→Render path.
  - **To name the culprit in one match: open the game with `?debug=net` on the phone.** `net-hud.js:176` then prints `<rtt>ms ±<jitter> · <snapRate>/s · gap <ms> · q<unacked> · <level>/<raw>:<reason>` — `reason` is the signal that fired.
  - ⚠️ **Prod is NOT running HEAD right now**: `curl -s .../shared/bot-ai.js | shasum -a 256` gives `e6f16f98…` vs HEAD's `ecc95468…`. The phone is on older bots than this repo — worth knowing before trusting any "the bots do X" report from a device.
  - Harness gotcha: `ping` is gated behind a `join` (`server.js:1247` `if (!member) return`), so an RTT probe that only opens a socket gets no pongs and looks like an unreachable server.

Left alone: `server.js`, `test-bot-ladder.mjs`, `scripts/build-arena-watch.mjs` (another agent extended my script with `--from=HEAD` + a smoke test — kept).

So this round **un-inverted** it: shipped state is goals rho 0.70, top beats bottom +0.53 vs +0.03, spread 48%, strips rho 0.90. **It still misses the 0.85 rho gate** — both facts are true, and reading only the failing gate would "fix" a ladder far better than the one we started with. Two graded knobs tried to close the gap and both measured worse or neutral (details in BOT_HANDOFF round 10); the remaining bottom-tier inversion is the deferred 12-level re-cut, not a tuning task.

- **Request (18:5x → 19:2x): "make a real artifact of a real bot fight, 2v2 AND 3v3, one that updates as the code updates" → then "maybe add a button in the artifact to refetch?"**
  - Extended the EXISTING watch page rather than forking a fourth one. Files taken: `scripts/arena-watch.template.html`, `scripts/build-arena-watch.mjs`, `public/_arena-watch.html` (locks taken; committed in `2e428fd`, `c810495`, `02e6c39`). Artifact published (URL is with the user); re-published to the same URL on each change.
  - **2v2 + 3v3.** The page could only ever play 2v2 — `IDS` was a fixed 4-entry list and `state.teamSize` was never set, so picking the שלושות layout gave FOUR bots on the 3v3 pitch. Now a format toggle mirroring `server.js` FORMATS (`quick` = teamSize 2 + main arena, `3v3` = teamSize 3 + FIELD_3V3). **`teamSize` must be set BEFORE `setField`/`addPlayer`** — sim.js plans the kickoff formation per slot, so setting it after spawns six bots into a 2v2 formation. Opens on 2v2-on-main (what ships) instead of a generated layout.
  - **"Updates as the code updates" — the honest answer.** A published artifact is served under a CSP that blocks every EXTERNAL host, so it can NEVER fetch `/shared` from prod or the LAN server: **a published page cannot self-update, full stop.** What was built instead: a **build stamp** (source, commit, subject, build time, sha256 fingerprint over the bundled module sources, per-module hashes, which shared modules were uncommitted) rendered in a "How current is this page?" block; **`--check <file>`** which exits 1 when the bundled code has changed (the hook for any automation); and a **"Check for a newer build" button** that asks the page's OWN origin for `/shared/<module>` — resolves when served BY the game server (CURRENT / BEHIND naming each changed module), says so plainly in the sandbox, and is disabled on the live-import dev twin because a reload IS the refetch. **No automation was scheduled** — the user was offered cron/git-hook/CC-hook and asked for the button instead.
  - **Two gotchas worth stealing:** (1) **hash BYTES, not decoded text** — hashing `r.text()` makes the answer depend on whether the response carried `charset=utf-8`, and these modules contain Hebrew, so identical bytes read as "every module changed"; (2) **FNV-1a, not sha256** — `crypto.subtle` is undefined outside a secure context and the dev surface is plain HTTP on a LAN IP, i.e. WebCrypto is missing exactly where the button must work.
  - **`--from=HEAD` + a SMOKE TEST added to the builder.** A publish must be reproducible from a commit, and this worktree frequently is not one: at 18:0x it carried `require()` in an ESM `server.js` (server would not boot) and an undefined `FROZEN_ARRIVAL_PENALTY` in `bot-ai.js` — both uncommitted, both invisible to a bundler that just reads files. The smoke test plays 30 s at BOTH team sizes over the chosen source and refuses to write if it throws.
  - **Play-by-play caught up with the bot work:** added `chaseCommit`, `pressIntercept`, `secondPress`, and an unknown tag is now announced AS a gap ("ran a play this page has no words for yet: X") instead of printing raw. The skill census gained no rows — its `ref` numbers are measured, and no census has run on the new tags.
  - Verified over CDP: 4 bots/2v2 on main, 6 bots/3v3 on threes, 6 on a generated layout, clock advancing in both, provenance rendered, no horizontal scroll, zero exceptions; button proven in all three contexts (file:// → "no live source", matching HEAD `/shared` → CURRENT across 9 modules, one-line-modified `bot-ai.js` → BEHIND naming only that module).
  - ⚠️ **Harness lesson:** the button's first run reported BEHIND for a matching build and **the page was right** — `shared/bot-ai.js` held 172 uncommitted lines from another agent while the page was built `--from=HEAD`. When a drift check "lies", check which tree you handed the test before you touch the code.
  - ⚠️ **Also: my `scripts/` edits vanished mid-session** (tree read clean, all markers gone) and reappeared minutes later — almost certainly another agent's stash/pop cycle around a shared checkout. Nothing was lost because the built artifact lived outside the repo. **Commit shared-file work the moment it verifies; do not hold it uncommitted while another agent is active.**

- **Request (18:2x): "read the handoffs in `summery/bots logic handoff` — no code, research only. 1) Is the bot THINKING option good? 2) What alternatives are there? 3) What are Brawl Stars and Fortnite doing?"**
  - **Research only. No file in `shared/`, `public/` or any test was touched.** Read all four docs in that folder + `bot-ai.js` architecture header + the refuted lists in `BOT_HANDOFF.md` §4. **Wrote the answer to `summery/bots logic handoff/ARCHITECTURE-REVIEW-2026-07-26.md`** — read that, not this bullet. Files taken (both new): that doc + `bot-cost.mjs`. `test-bot-ladder.mjs` was already modified by another agent and was left alone.
  - New instrument **`bot-cost.mjs`** — what does bot thinking COST, and can we afford to think ahead? MAIN_FIELD, 4 bots, seeded, 6×60s of LIVE ticks: **`computeBotInputs` 1.24 µs/tick for 4 bots (0.31 µs per bot); `step()` 4.25 µs (3.4× the thinking); full tick 5.84 µs = 0.035% of a 60 Hz tick** (per-match 5.47-6.33). `structuredClone(state)` 36.5 µs, JSON round-trip 20.0 µs. **Thinking costs the same at skill 0.82 and 0.93 and on every arena; `step()` swings 4.25 µs on main vs 1.21 µs on the bare arena — the GEOMETRY is what costs.** ⇒ a **rollout planner is affordable**: 8 candidates × 4 bots replanned at 10 Hz ≈ **2% of a tick**, and the **state COPY, not the sim, is the dominant cost**.
  - ⚠️ Two harness traps baked into that tool's header: **a finished match is FREE** (`step()` early-returns; v1 of the bench ran 20k ticks on a dead state and reported `step()` at 0.0 µs — the celebration-freeze trap again, third time this session), and **the timer is not free** at ~1 µs granularity (splits are net of a measured 0.02 µs/call). **One room on a dev Mac — divide by concurrent rooms and re-measure on Render before quoting the %.**
  - Verdict recorded: the branch-chain controller is good and nearly out of headroom **as a single layer**; the two structural leaks are (a) a **shadow physics model** in `bot-ai.js` that keeps diverging from `sim.js` (roll range, interception radius, glue-spot launch origin, lead speed, `laneClear`, wall reflection, `pointInBush`) and (b) **no first-class plan object**, so a wind-up is not owned by the branch that armed it. Both are the same recommendation the two handoffs reach from opposite directions.
  - Brawl Stars / Fortnite research: neither publishes bot internals; what is public is behaviour + engine frameworks (UE **StateTree**/BT/EQS, navmesh, and **UEFN Guard Spawner**'s actual shipped knobs — `Visibility Range`, `Accuracy`, `Max Patrol Distance`, `Team Awareness Propagation`, unaware/suspicious/alerted states). **Both AAA answers put difficulty in perception + accuracy + bot COUNT (matchmaker), not in smarter planning** — which is the same conclusion this repo reached by deleting `decisionHz` seven times. Rocket League is the better comparison for a ball game: its bot ecosystem ships a shared **ball-prediction oracle** (nobody re-derives physics) and its strong agents are **RL-trained** (Necto/Nexto ≈ GC1).

- **⚠️ CORRECTED: the push cadence is ROUTINE, not an anomaly — but prod DOES autodeploy, which `CLAUDE.md` says it does not (agent `wall-windup`, 17:1x, corrected 17:2x)** — I originally logged this as an "unrequested push + deploy". **The fuller data says that framing was wrong, and the correction is the point of this entry.**
  - **What I got wrong, and how.** I ran `git reflog show origin/main | head -12` and reported the earliest *visible* entry as the start of the cadence ("12 pushes, 00:58 → 17:07"). `pass-direction` caught the truncation and re-counted as 14 since 00:43:39 — also truncated. **The untruncated reflog has 117 `update by push` entries spanning 2026-07-19 14:25:35 → 2026-07-26 17:07:34**: 20 · 9 · 31 · 11 · 13 · 5 · 11 · **17 today**, today's earliest at **00:02:17**. Lesson worth more than the finding: *never report a range off a `head`-limited log* — two agents independently drew a conclusion from a window and both understated the same number.
  - **So this is the repo's normal operating mode**, 5–31 pushes a day for eight days, long predating every agent session. Not a rogue agent, and nothing here needs undoing. My "🚨 unrequested" heading was alarmist; this replaces it.
  - **The one thing that IS still worth the user's attention, unchanged:** `curl https://pikme-football.onrender.com/shared/bot-ai.js` is **byte-identical** (226,623 bytes) to pushed tip `866a116`, so **the game service is deploying from pushes** — while `CLAUDE.md` states `srv-d9ebcvtaeets73ar91sg` has a dead webhook and needs the CLI. That line is what makes agents treat a game-repo push as harmless, and the evidence contradicts it. **I did not edit `CLAUDE.md`**: it holds the user's standing rules, and correcting it is their call — not an agent's, and explicitly not on another agent's request. `pass-direction` independently reached the same position and also left it alone.
  - **The repo's own record makes the contradiction sharper, and splits it by SERVICE.** ⚠️ **Cited by QUOTED TEXT, not line number — this log is PREPENDED to by several agents a day, so line refs rot within hours** (my first version of this bullet cited lines 246/270/432; within the hour those pointed at a blank line, a 3v3 MODES bullet and the Brawl-Stars rule). **And the rule caught me one more time on the way out:** verifying whether a peer's claim was true, I ran a case-SENSITIVE `git grep` for a phrase the file holds in capitals, got zero hits, and was one keystroke from reporting a true claim as unverified. A grep pattern is a window too — `-i`, and check the negative result before trusting it. Grep for `srv-chgb1k67avjbbju8aoig` has \`autoDeploy: yes\` — the **API** autodeploying, a ~03:00 push live by 03:11, which matches `CLAUDE.md`. Grep for "ships nothing" / "does NOT autodeploy" — the opposite claim for the **GAME** service (`srv-d9ebcvtaeets73ar91sg`), CLI required (5 separate entries say it). That is the claim my measurement contradicts: prod's `shared/bot-ai.js` is byte-identical to the pushed tip. Two explanations remain and I cannot separate them without touching the user's Render account: **the game webhook is alive again, or someone ran `render deploys create`.** Either way the documented assumption is unsafe.
  - ✅ **RESOLVED BY A THIRD AGENT, which is the right outcome.** `CLAUDE.md:12` was rewritten in `0314eda` → `8e35ad2` → `af1b944` (none of them mine or `pass-direction`'s — we both declined to edit it, independently, and were right to: the evidence was good enough, the authority was not ours). I verified the final text and it is **accurate and properly narrow**: it keeps the API autodeploy as confirmed, states only what was measured for the game service (`curl … | shasum -a 256` byte-identical to local HEAD after a push), and `af1b944` explicitly narrows it to *"a matching hash proves STATE, not mechanism"* — i.e. it does NOT claim to know whether the webhook revived or someone ran the CLI. That is exactly the distinction I could not resolve and did not claim.
  - ⚠️ **Do NOT cite `9339cee` ("chore: re-probe the Render webhook") as evidence about the webhook** — its message does not describe its content. Lines **265** and **360** of this log independently record another agent's 3v3 client work being swept into it, and its diff is match-info/bot-dossier/3v3 feature work with nothing webhook-related in it. A commit title is not a measurement; this is the same window-error as a truncated reflog, in a third costume.

- **🧱 L10 "there are still idle moments" — THE DOMINANT CAUSE WAS THE MEASURING TOOL, and the wall wind-up had a real bug underneath it (agent `wall-windup`, 15:0x–16:0x)** — Files taken: `bot-idle.mjs`, `shared/bot-ai.js` (wall-windup lines only; lock `football-mock:shared/bot-ai.js` held).
  - **The reported idle does not exist.** `bot-idle.mjs` called a bot "stationary" below **1.2 px/tick**. A full-speed walk is `158 × 0.9 speedMul = 142.2 px/s = 2.37 px/tick`, and `BUILD_WINDUP_SLOW 0.5` (`sim.js:715`) halves it to **1.185 px/tick** — the threshold sat 0.015px above the wind-up crawl. Measured: while `buildHold` the displacement is **1.18 / 1.19 / 1.19 px/tick at p10/p50/p90** (a flat walk, `mean|move| = 1.00`, not a stall), and a threshold sweep gives 3.25 episodes/match at 1.2px → 0.38 at 1.0px → **0.00 at 0.8px**. **No bot at L10 is stationary for 0.3s.** A human pays the identical slow winding up their own wall, so this is the game's own rule working.
  - Fixed the instrument: `STOP_PX` (default 0.8, overridable to sweep), the arithmetic written into the header so nobody re-derives it, and a separate **SLOWED but walking** report. That now reads: carrying the ball 15.9 s/match · rubbing along geometry/bodies 11.5 s/match · **wall wind-up only 1.5 s/match**. STOPPED is now 0.89% of bot-ticks with a longest stopped run of **0.28 s**.
  - The 28% "blocked, no tag" slice, re-measured under 1.0 px/tick: **1.90 s/match total**, of which **52% is knockback recovery** (the bot is being blasted — correct), 33% enemy body, 11% mate body, and only **0.38 episodes/match ≥0.3 s**. Round 6's body avoidance did its job; nothing left to fix there.
  - **The real bug the wind-up was hiding:** `bm.buildHold` only ever stored a `dist`, never the wall's aim, so the wall was placed with whatever aim the branch running on the COMMIT tick supplied — and that is a different branch (`blockDrive` arms and sets `bm.trap` on the same tick, so the bot spends the wind-up as `ambushStrip` aiming at the carrier: per-tick tags over one wind-up were blockDrive 10 vs ambushStrip 260). Measured drift at commit: **38° median on `blockDrive`, 116° on `catapultWall`**, placement error up to 152px. So the branches pinned to their own feet "so the wall doesn't move" were paying the slow for nothing.
  - Fix: latch `wx`/`wy` + the wall normal into `bm.buildHold`; `finalize()` re-derives `buildDist` from the live position every tick (as the client's drag ghost does), owns the emitted aim for the wind-up, never commits a wall on a tick the bot also fires (one aim, two jobs) with a 0.25s cap so the deferral can't hang, drops a wind-up the instant the bot picks the ball up (same invariant as `bombHold`), and LEASHES the steering target into the window where the wall still lands — only for tiers whose `wallReach` has push distance to give.
  - **Outcome, 24 matches × 60s, L10:** walls raised against an enemy carrier miss perpendicular-to-the-lane by a median **22° → 4°** (p90 63° → 5°) and **64% → 100%** are within 30° of spanning the lane; commit aim drift **38°/116° → 0°**; wall builds **2.2 → 3.9/match** (UP, so this is not "idle removed by never building").
  - **Measured and REFUTED, do not re-propose:** *aborting* a wind-up that leaves the window (every `ambushWall` hold aborted, builds 1.75 → **0.38**/match — the strip wins the ball, our team then owns it, and `wallSpotOk`'s own-lane test flips: the trigger destroys its own precondition); *gating the arm on standing in the window* (`blockDrive` 1.25 → **0.13** arms/match, its stand spot is recomputed from the carrier's live position so the bot chases a moving target — the walk-to-a-pad autopsy for the third time); and *leashing every tier* (below `T_FAR_WALL` the along-window is a single distance, so the leash is a freeze: skill 0.50 worst jam 0.97 → 1.61 s).
  - **Wall builds checked against the "did you fix idle by never building?" trap** with `p.stat.walls` (exact — incremented inside `buildWall`), 4 seed bases × 12 × 60s: **3.34 → 3.65/match, +9%.** They build as often as before, and every wall now spans what it was aimed at.
  - QA: paired A/B against a pristine `git archive HEAD` copy (the other agent's in-flight `shootTgt`/pass-latch work was filtered out of my patch so it could not skew the numbers), 3 seed bases × 12 matches × 60s. At skill **0.93** worst jam 0.68 → 0.59 s, jams>0.5s 0.14 → 0.11/match, reach-timeouts 4.00 → 3.61/match, backward releases **24.7% → 17.9%** (round 7's open item 3), touches 21.6 → 22.3. At skill 0.50 the deltas sit inside the noise band `CLAUDE.md` already records for this instrument (identical code has reported pinning 0.27–0.51%).
  - **Cross-checked against `d01d3b8` (`pass-direction`'s pass abort clears `bm.charging`).** They flagged it in case my wind-up latch keyed off `bm.charging` surviving across ticks. It does not — the latch keys off `bm.buildHold`; `bm.charging` only reaches it through `fire`, and my commit-deferral is `if (past && (!fire || past+0.25))`, so clearing `bm.charging` makes `fire` false and the wall commit **sooner**, never later. Measured on the combined HEAD, 3 seed bases × 12 × 60s: mean hold **0.598 / 0.611 / 0.611 s** against the 0.600 s design target (`BUILD_WINDUP + 0.1`) — nothing hangs. Outcome survives the combination: 22° → **3°** median miss from perpendicular-to-lane, 64% → **94%** spanning, 52 → 95 builds over 24 matches. The 0.13 aborts/match now visible are my own carrier invariant firing (a bot winding up a wall picked the ball up), which is what it is for.
  - **RE-MEASURED AGAINST REAL HEAD (`8e0b069`), after `pass-direction` correctly pointed out that all three of us were A/B-ing against our own frozen bases at the same time — so my `d01d3b8` numbers were taken on a tree that no longer exists.** Method changed to the one that answers the right question: current HEAD vs **HEAD with only my wall-windup hunks reverse-applied** (`git apply -R` of `176f525`, clean). Every claim reproduces, and the defect is WORSE on the integrated tree than on the base I found it on:
    - aim drift at commit, HEAD-minus-mine: `blockDrive` 14° · `ambushWall` 17° · `screenWall` 40° · `deflectSetup` 79° · **`catapultWall` 156°**, placement error up to **206px**. With my change: **0° on every branch.**
    - walls raised against an enemy carrier (24 × 60s, bigger sample than before): miss from perpendicular-to-the-lane **22° → 4°** median, **78° → 6°** p90; **64% → 97%** within 30° of spanning the lane; 25 → 36 such walls of 68 → 91 builds.
    - builds/match by the exact `p.stat.walls` counter, 4 seed bases: **3.52 → 3.84, +9%** — the same +9% I measured on the old base, so the "did you fix idle by never building?" check holds.
    - mean hold **0.613 / 0.620 s** against the 0.600 s design target — the anti-hang cross-check for `bm.charging` survives the re-do (`427e16e` leaves that reset in place).
    - the instrument claim on real HEAD: STOPPED **0.87%** of bot-ticks, longest stopped run **0.43 s**, and `buildHold` no longer appears in the STOPPED list at all; the wind-up is 1.8 s/match in the SLOWED bucket.
  - **`test-rank-parity` is NOT flaky — answered for `pass-direction`, who saw it fail.** It passes **6/6** here and hard-fails in a scratch checkout, by design: `test-rank-parity.mjs:30` requires the sibling `../pikme-server/data/football-rank.js` and refuses to self-disable ("a check that turns itself off is not a check"). A `git archive` copy has no sibling repo, so it fails there and only there. Nothing to fix.

Pooled HEAD 61/76 = 80% vs mine 61/83 = 74%, inside one binomial SE (~4.7pp at n≈80). Threshold NOT lowered: (i) is now a printed tripwire with that spread quoted beside it — the same call §3 records for `test-bot-ladder`'s shot count and round 6 made for this test's own absolute rate. The gates that carry the cannon fix are untouched and pass. That makes three instruments in this repo now known to be past their resolution limit.

My code landed in **`176f525`**. **Attribution corrected after `pass-direction` wrote back:** the `shootTgt`/`tgtId`/`latchDead`/`gainedBall` bullet-target latch I saw in the tree is **`bullet-aim`'s**, shipped as **`62f9a10`** — not theirs; `pass-direction`'s change was the `receivePass` block + `PASS_BACK_COS` + the backward-pass abort, shipped as **`d01d3b8`**.
